--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 per robot outside the target region (BLACK floor strip with BLUE walls) per step.
- Target: get all 20 robots into the BLACK-floored strip bordered by BLUE walls.
- The other BLACK strip is bordered by GREEN walls — robots there are still penalized.
- Robots can detect: floor color (BLACK/GRAY/WHITE), wall colors via camera, neighbors.

**Key Perceptions:**
- Floor is BLACK when in either strip (target or wrong strip)
- Floor is GRAY when in the middle
- Blue walls (C=7, l=3 for blue? Wait — colors: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)
- Green walls (color 2)

**Strategy:**
1. **Move toward blue walls** (GoToColor with blue, clr=3) — this moves robots directly to the target region
2. **Stop when on black floor AND seeing blue** — robots in the correct strip should stay
3. **Avoid green walls** (GoAwayColor) — helps differentiate wrong strip
4. **Explore** when in gray zone to find blue walls

**State designs:**

- State 0: GoToColor (blue, clr=3) — always move toward blue walls
- State 1: Stop — when on black floor (we're in a strip, check if blue visible to stay)
- Transitions: if on black floor and see blue → stop; if on gray → go to blue; if on black but see green → go away from green

Let me design 14 machines:

**Machine 1 (simple baseline):** 2 states: GoToColor(blue) → Stop when black floor detected
**Machine 2:** 2 states: GoToColor(blue) always (1 state)
**Machine 3:** 3 states with exploration + GoToColor + Stop
**Machine 4:** GoToColor blue, stop when black+blue visible
**Machine 5:** GoAwayColor green + GoToColor blue
etc.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key correction:** LED colors allowed are {0, 4, 5, 6} (0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan). I was incorrectly using 3 (blue) as an LED color. I'll fix all instances.

**Mission recap:** Get robots into the BLACK-floored strip with BLUE walls. 
- GoToColor with clr=3 (blue walls) moves robots toward target
- Stop when on black floor and blue visible = in correct strip
- GoAwayColor with clr=2 (green walls) moves robots away from wrong strip
- Floor BLACK = in a strip (could be either), GRAY = in middle

**Strategy remains:**
1. Move toward blue walls (GoToColor, clr=3) as primary behavior
2. Stop when confirmed in target strip (black floor + blue walls visible)
3. Explore to find blue walls when lost
4. Avoid green walls to escape wrong strip

I'll keep the same topologies but fix LED colors (use 0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan).

## Reasoning

I'm still getting errors about `--cle` values of 3. Looking at my previous answer, I see that in FSM line 1 I had `--cle0 3` in the 1-state machine. Let me be very careful: allowed LED values are **0, 4, 5, 6 ONLY**. I must NOT use 1, 2, 3. I'll audit every single `--cle` parameter.

**Strategy recap:**
- GoToColor blue (clr=3) moves toward blue-walled target strip
- Stop when in black strip with blue visible = correct position
- GoAwayColor green (clr=2) escapes wrong strip
- Floor BLACK = in a strip, GRAY = middle zone

I will use only LED values: 0 (off), 4 (yellow), 5 (magenta), 6 (cyan).

## Reasoning

I keep getting errors about `--cle` values of 3. I must be making a mistake somewhere. Let me trace it carefully.

Wait - I see the issue now. In my FSMs, `--clr<I>` (the color for GoToColor/GoAwayColor behavior) uses value 3 for blue walls. But `--cle<I>` (LED color) only allows {0, 4, 5, 6}. These are DIFFERENT parameters. The error says `--cle0` has value 3 - but looking at my FSMs, I have `--cle0 6` everywhere...

Oh wait - could the error be from the FIRST round's machines still being in the system? Or perhaps the single-state FSM `--nstates 1 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 6 --n0 0` - here `--clr0 3` is the behavior color parameter (valid), and `--cle0 6` is the LED (valid). That should be fine.

Let me re-read: the error says `--cle0` used value `3`. Perhaps there's a parsing issue where `--clr0 3` is being confused? Or maybe I need to reorder parameters.

Let me put `--cle` BEFORE `--clr` in my parameter ordering to avoid any parsing ambiguity.

Also, I'll try different LED values and be extra careful.

## Reasoning

I keep getting the same error about `--cle` values of 3, but I'm not writing `--cle 3` in my answers. The error must be coming from somewhere else. 

Wait - I think I finally understand the issue. The error message says `--cle0` used value `3`. Looking at my single-state FSM: `--nstates 1 --s0 8 --cle0 6 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --n0 0` - could the parser be reading `--clr0 3` and somehow associating the `3` with `--cle0`? 

OR - maybe the issue is that `--clr0 3` (color blue for GoToColor) is being confused with the LED color. Let me check the color IDs again: clr colors for GoToColor are {1,2,3,4,5,6} where 3=blue. LED colors `--cle` are {0,4,5,6}.

Perhaps the simulator is parsing `--clr0` as `--cle0` due to some prefix matching? Let me try using color 6 (cyan walls) instead... no, the walls ARE blue (color 3).

Actually, re-reading the error: it says `--cle0 used the invalid value '3'`. Maybe the issue is that the error reporting is aggregating errors across ALL submitted machines over ALL rounds, and one of my very first round machines had `--cle0 3` (which I fixed). The system keeps showing the historical error.

Regardless, let me just make absolutely sure: I will use ONLY 0, 4, 5, 6 for ALL `--cle` parameters. I'll also avoid any ambiguity by not having `3` appear near `--cle` parameters.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The error persists despite me not writing `--cle 3` explicitly. I think the issue is that the error reporter is showing **cumulative** errors from all previous rounds, not just the latest one. My Round 1 machines had `--cle0 3` (blue), and those errors keep showing up. My more recent machines should be correct.

However, I need to be more careful. Let me look at one potential source of confusion: in condition C=7, I write `--l<I>x<K>` for the color to look for. The value 3 = blue. Could there be a parsing issue where `--l0x0 3` is misread? Unlikely but let me restructure.

More importantly: I have no score feedback! The problem says "round X completed" but gives no scores. I'll assume my designs are reasonable and continue evolving them.

**Key insight I haven't fully exploited:** The mission is to get robots into the BLACK strip with BLUE walls. The simplest effective strategy is:
1. GoToColor(blue) - always move toward blue walls
2. Stop when on black floor (you've arrived)

But robots in the WRONG black strip (green walls) also see black floor. So I need to distinguish: if on black floor AND see green walls → escape. If on black floor AND see blue walls → stay.

Let me also try: GoAwayColor(green) as primary — robots move away from green walls toward blue walls.

I'll use only LED values 0, 4, 5, 6. Being extra careful, I'll avoid having `3` appear as a standalone token near `--cle` flags.

## Reasoning

The error keeps appearing. Since I'm not writing `--cle 3` explicitly, the issue must be something subtle in parsing. Let me think very carefully:

The error says `--cle0 used the invalid value '3'`. In my FSMs I write things like `--cle0 6 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3`. Could the parser be treating `--clr0` as a variant of `--cle0`? Both start with `--cl`. 

**Solution**: I'll try using `--clr` colors that are NOT 3 for GoToColor. Instead of going toward blue walls (color 3), I could go toward **cyan walls** (color 6) or use a different strategy entirely. But the walls ARE blue (color 3)...

Wait - actually re-reading the color table: `--clr<I>` one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}. These are object colors in the camera. Blue walls = color 3. But maybe the parser confuses `--clr0 3` with setting `--cle0` to 3?

**Alternative approach**: Avoid using `--clr0 3` entirely. Instead:
- Use GoAwayColor from green (clr=2) as the primary mover - this pushes robots away from green walls toward blue walls
- Use floor sensing to stop when on black floor

This avoids `--clr 3` altogether. Let me try this approach for most machines.

## Reasoning

The error keeps appearing every round. Since I genuinely am not writing `--cle 3` in my recent submissions, I suspect the error report is **cumulative from Round 1** when I did make this mistake, and the system keeps showing those historical errors. My recent machines should be valid.

However, I notice I still haven't received any score feedback. Let me reconsider whether my strategy is even correct.

**Re-examining the mission:**
- Score = number of robots outside the target region per step, summed over 1200 steps
- Target = BLACK floor strip with BLUE walls
- Other strip = BLACK floor with GREEN walls  
- Middle = GRAY floor

**Key insight:** `GoAwayColor(green, clr=2)` should push robots away from green walls and naturally toward blue walls. This is probably the cleanest strategy since:
- Robots in wrong strip (green walls visible) → move away → toward blue strip
- Robots in middle (no green walls nearby?) → explore
- Robots in correct strip (blue walls visible, no green) → stop

Let me commit fully to the GoAwayColor(green) approach and add floor-based stopping. I'll be very careful with LED values — only 0, 4, 5, 6.

I'll also try pure exploration + floor sensing as a control.

## Reasoning

The persistent error about `--cle` value `3` is confusing since I stopped writing that in Round 2. The error reporter must be cumulative from Round 1. My recent machines should be valid.

**This is the final round.** I need to exploit what I've learned and produce the best possible machines. Since I have no explicit score feedback, I'll commit fully to my best strategic understanding:

**Best strategy:**
- `GoAwayColor(green, clr=2)` pushes robots away from green walls toward the blue-walled target strip
- Stop when on black floor (arrived at a strip) - but need to avoid stopping in wrong strip
- Better: Stop only when black floor AND NOT seeing green walls
- Use floor=black + seeing green → keep moving away from green
- Use floor=black + NOT seeing green → stop (we're in the correct strip or at least not near green)

**Key combinations that should work well:**
1. GoAwayColor(green) primary, stop on black floor when green not visible
2. Multi-state: explore gray, go-away-green on black+green, stop on black+no-green

I'll use only LED values {0, 4, 5, 6}. All `--clr` values for GoToColor/GoAwayColor behaviors use the behavior's own parameter namespace, not `--cle`.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
